@@138260520 <h> Addressing Nigeria 's environmental challenges <p> At an event to commemorate this year 's World Environment Day , the federal government observed that climate change is a major threat to sustainable development . Director of the Department of Climate Change , Federal Ministry of Environment , Iniobong Abiola-Awe , who stated this at an event to mark the WED organised by the Shehu Musa Yar'Adua Foundation in Abuja , hinted that developing countries , such as Nigeria , are particularly vulnerable to climate-related risks due to rapid population growth , urbanisation pressures , infrastructure deficits and other developmental challenges . <p> Nigeria is among gas-flaring countries in the world despite measures to curb the menace . Available figures showed that Nigeria lost about $56.75billion to gas flaring between 2002 and 2024 , with cumulative green gas emission exceeding 714 million tonnes of carbon dioxide equivalent . Data from the World Bank indicated that Nigeria flared about 5.3 cubic metres of gas in 2022 across 174 flare sites . <p> Besides , the World Health Organisation ( WHO ) has revealed that Nigeria recorded an estimated 175 deaths per 100,000 @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ World Bank , on irs part , said that ambient air pollution caused about 11,200 premature deaths in 2018 in Lagos . The theme of the 2026 World Environment Day , " Inspired by Nature , For Climate , For Our Future , " marks a critical shift from incremental promises to urgent legally binding climate action . It is also a clarion call on governments to scale up action to address their environmental challenges . <p> Marking the day , the United Nations ( UN ) Secretary-General , Antonio Guteress , warned that the earth is " sending distress signals everywhere , " citing the past 11 years as the warmest on record . He also called for urgent action to limit the 1.5-degree temperature overshoot , slash emissions and accelerate the transition to renewable energy . <p> Similarly , the United Nations Environment Programme ( UNEP ) stated that the earth is sending urgent signals @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ heat -- and called for immediate global climate action under the campaign theme #Now For Climate . It also targets net-zero emissions by 2060 . The government is working hard to achieve the 17-point UN Sustainable Development Goals ( SDGs ) by prioritising the eradication of poverty , safeguard the environment and ensure shared prosperity by 2030 . internal routes . <p> However , an extensive investigation by Sunday PUNCH revealed more troubling challenges than those commonly speculated about and which authorities have largely kept away from public scrutiny . <p> Findings showed that ageing equipment , frequent machinery breakdowns , deteriorating internal roads , and operational bottlenecks have combined to slow waste disposal activities , resulting in the long queues witnessed daily by commuters and residents along the corridor . <p> Concerned by the increasing volume of refuse on roads and streets across Lagos , coupled with the Collapsing road <p> Poor condition of dumpsite 's roads <p> For many truck drivers and waste collectors , accessing the Olusosun landfill has become a daily ordeal . Long queues of refuse trucks now snake around the facility as operators wait for hours , and sometimes days , to gain entry and offload waste . <p> Speaking with our correspondent , Ibrahim ( surname withheld ) , a truck conductor who regularly transports refuse to the site , said the landfill is operating beyond its optimal capacity and struggling to cope with the volume of waste generated daily across Lagos . <p> He explained that the situation is compounded by the poor state of the machinery and vehicles used in managing waste at the facility . <p> " Most of the equipment being used there is old and obsolete . sanitation concerns . <p> The task at hand <p> For decades , the Olusosun landfill , regarded as Africa 's largest dumpsite , has served as a major destination for waste generated across Lagos . <p> However , increasing concerns about its capacity and deteriorating operational condition have fuelled calls for urgent intervention . <p> Stakeholders warn that without substantial investment in modern equipment and alternative waste-treatment solutions , the challenges currently affecting the facility could worsen , resulting in longer delays , higher operational costs for waste operators , and heightened environmental and public health risks . <p> Environmental experts say the problems at Olusosun reflect broader challenges facing waste management infrastructure in Lagos , where rapid @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in the volume of municipal waste generated daily . <p> They stressed the need for modern waste management facilities , including improved recycling systems , waste-to-energy projects and the rehabilitation of existing landfill infrastructure . <p> Sunday PUNCH learnt that when the landfill was established in the 1950s , it was located on the outskirts of Lagos and was designed to receive waste from both individual and corporate sources . <p> However , decades of rapid urban expansion have transformed the surrounding landscape , with residential estates , commercial centres , markets and industrial facilities springing up around the landfill . <p> This growth has brought a mounting environmental burden that continues to threaten the health and well-being of residents across the Lagos metropolis . <p> Studies indicate that people living within a five-kilometre radius of landfill sites face elevated health risks . <p> Respiratory symptoms have been reported among residents living near waste disposal facilities , largely due to exposure to endotoxins , microorganisms and aerosols generated during waste collection and landfilling activities . <p> Other hazards associated with living close to landfill sites include fires and explosions , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ around waste dumps , diseases spread by mosquitoes , flies and rodents , as well as the degradation of plant and wildlife habitats . <p> A recent World Health Organisation report warned that poorly managed solid waste is fuelling a public health crisis and called for urgent action to protect people and the environments in which they live . <p> According to the global health body , municipal solid waste is increasing at an unprecedented rate worldwide , yet many countries still lack the infrastructure and resources needed to manage it safely . <p> In the report , the Acting Director of the WHO Department of Environment , Climate Change , One Health and Migration , Dr Ruediger Krech , said , " Solid waste reflects how our societies produce and consume , and how we treat people and the environment in the process . <p> " If we continue to treat waste as an afterthought , we will lock in avoidable disease , climate pollution , and deep social inequities . This report is a clear call to put health and equity at the centre of how we design @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ The report summarised evidence showing how solid waste , particularly municipal waste , affects human health through contaminated air , water , soil , and food . It noted that when waste is not collected , or is dumped , burned , or poorly treated , it can release hazardous chemicals , pollute drinking-water sources , and create breeding grounds for disease-carrying insects and rodents . <p> " Communities underserved by waste management services , those living near dumpsites and poorly managed landfills and incinerators , children and pregnant women , as well as waste workers , particularly those working informally , face the greatest risks . <p> " For the water , sanitation , and hygiene community , the report underlines that safe management of municipal and healthcare waste is integral to safe , climate-resilient systems . <p> " A large share of municipal solid waste is still not collected or is disposed of in uncontrolled conditions , including open dumpsites and open burning . These practices damage ecosystems , contribute to climate change , and undermine efforts to build healthy cities , " the WHO stated . <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , Hygiene and Health Unit at WHO , Mr Bruce Gordon , said , " This report gives countries and health authorities a very practical agenda . Health ministries can start now by ensuring safe management of healthcare waste . These concrete steps save lives today and will make cities cleaner and healthier in the future . " <p> Studies have also linked communities around landfill sites to increased cases of water-borne diseases such as typhoid , dysentery , cholera , and other health conditions associated with poor environmental sanitation . <p> Sunday PUNCH learnt that concerns over these health and environmental risks prompted former Lagos State Governor , Akinwunmi Ambode , to shut down the dumpsite in March 2018 , citing the danger it posed to residents and traders living within the area . <p> While announcing the closure , he said , " The closure of the site became necessary owing to the need to forestall future occurrences and protect the health of residents . " <p> However , before the closure , a major fire outbreak caused by a flare had occurred at the facility . Such @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ accumulation of decomposing waste frequently generates combustible gases like methane . <p> According to an environmentalist and writer , Jarikre Johnson , who is known for his work on the Olusosun dumpsite , such incidents range from minor surface fires to massive infernos capable of releasing dangerous pollutants into the atmosphere . <p> He explained that emissions from such fires may include formaldehyde , hydrogen cyanide , hydrogen sulphide , and nitrogen oxides , all of which pose serious health risks to people living nearby . <p> Sunday PUNCH reports that following the shutdown , the state government directed waste operators to utilise alternative landfill sites at Owu-Ikosi in Ikorodu and Epe . <p> The closure formed part of the Cleaner Lagos Initiative , a programme designed to create a cleaner , safer , and healthier environment through a harmonised and holistic waste management system . <p> However , the initiative was short-lived . Allegations of sabotage and political disagreements within the new arrangement eventually led to the government 's decision to reopen the Olusosun dumpsite . Refuse compactor trucks on queue . <p> Shut for maintenance <p> In another @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ optimal capacity , the Lagos State Waste Management Authority shut down the facility for 48 hours in November 2021 to carry out maintenance works . <p> The closure , which took effect on November 10 , 2021 , was announced by the then Managing Director of LAWMA , Ibrahim Odumboni , who said the exercise was aimed at improving waste management operations at the facility . <p> In a statement , Odumboni explained that the temporary closure was necessary to undertake palliative works and ensure the efficient maintenance of landfills across the state . <p> " In a bid to ensure that waste management operations at the Olusosun dumpsite are effectively delivered , we will shut down the landfill for a period of 48 hours , starting from Wednesday , November 10 , 2021 , for maintenance , " he said . <p> Draining trip to the dumpsite <p> For many waste truck drivers , what was once a routine trip to the dumpsite has become a draining and time-consuming exercise that can last several days . <p> A driver in his mid-40s , identified simply as Rafiu , said @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ stretch into one or two days as trucks line up in long queues waiting for access to the offloading points . <p> According to him , the delays have significantly reduced the capacity of operators to evacuate waste from homes , markets , and business districts across Lagos . <p> " Large sections of the access roads have deteriorated , making it difficult for heavy-duty compactors to move freely . <p> " During the rainy season , like we are experiencing now , trucks become trapped in muddy sections , while some vehicles suffer mechanical damage after navigating the rough terrain . <p> " Equipment used to push , spread , and compress refuse is also inadequate and breaks down frequently , " he said . <p> Rafiu added that whenever the machines are unavailable or operating below capacity , waste accumulates faster than it can be processed , slowing the movement of trucks waiting to discharge . <p> " The congestion has left many trucks stranded for extended periods . Vehicles that arrive at the dumpsite in the morning may not complete offloading until the following day . <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ are available to collect waste from communities , leading to the accumulation of refuse in several parts of Lagos , " he added . <p> Similarly , another operator , who spoke on condition of anonymity for fear of victimisation , described the situation as dire , saying there was currently little available space for trucks to discharge refuse . <p> " The Lagos State Government manages the area where we offload refuse . We are PSP operators , but the facility itself is run by the government . <p> " Right now , we are facing serious challenges accessing the dumpsite because of the damaged roads . <p> " Sometimes trucks get stuck in the mud , and if you are not careful , the vehicle can even tip over . When that happens , you are left to bear the consequences yourself . <p> " We have experienced many incidents like that . I am not even sure whether we will be able to offload at the dumpsite today , " he said . <p> Another operator , Fola , said the challenge was not a shortage of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ because of the poor condition of the internal roads leading to the dumping points . <p> " My three trucks are currently at the dumpsite and have not returned for us to continue operations . <p> " Even after gaining access , trucks still have to navigate difficult terrain before reaching the discharge points . <p> " Sometimes the roads are so bad that vehicles struggle to move through them . About three months ago , my truck 's engine was damaged because of the bad roads within the facility . This has seriously affected our operations , " he said . <p> Inadequate payloaders <p> Another driver , Olamide , told our correspondent that inadequate pay-loaders and the poor condition of those available were further contributing to the delays . <p> He explained that the machines frequently fail to push and compress waste efficiently , slowing the discharge process and worsening congestion . <p> According to him , there was a time when trucks could offload their waste within minutes , but that is no longer the case . <p> He also alleged irregularities in the queue management system @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ waiting lines while others remain stranded for days . <p> " At night , some people find ways around the system . If you are connected , you may get through quickly . If you are not , you remain in the queue , " the driver alleged . <p> The cost of delays <p> The operational challenges at the dumpsite are already having visible consequences across Lagos . <p> In many communities , heaps of refuse that would ordinarily have been evacuated now remain unattended , creating unsightly surroundings and increasing environmental pollution . <p> Residents said waste evacuation trucks had not visited the area for about two weeks , forcing many households to rely on outlawed cart pushers , while others resorted to dumping their refuse along roadsides . <p> A resident of Ijesha Road , Mercy Chinemere , said she had been forced to pay cart pushers to dispose of her household waste because collection trucks no longer visited the area regularly . <p> She said , " The truck has not come for about two weeks , so I have been paying cart pushers to clear @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ want to pay dump their waste along the expressway because LAWMA trucks used to evacuate it . They have not come for weeks now . " <p> Experts ' opinion <p> Experts warn that poor waste management at the dumpsite could trigger serious environmental and public health consequences . <p> An epidemiologist and public health physician at the University of Ilorin Teaching Hospital , Kwara State , Prof Tanimola Akande , said poorly managed waste sites could contribute to airborne and respiratory diseases among residents living near the dumpsite . <p> He added that waste accumulation in residential areas could attract rodents that transmit Lassa fever , thereby exposing residents to infection . <p> Akande , a former National President of the Association of Public Health Physicians of Nigeria , said waste management remains a major challenge in the country , beginning from the point of waste generation in homes . <p> According to him , waste is often not properly sorted or disposed of in appropriate bins , while segregation is virtually non-existent . <p> " The waste is commonly dumped in open spaces and on streets , and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ streets and open spaces become filled with refuse , creating health hazards . <p> " Rodents and insects such as mosquitoes breed in such environments . Rainwater and floodwater often wash germs from these locations into surrounding areas , including drinking-water sources . <p> " Rodents like rats , which transmit diseases , also thrive in these places . Final waste disposal and treatment remain major challenges in many Nigerian cities . Yes , rodents breeding around waste sites can transmit diseases , including Lassa fever , " he said . <p> Ways out <p> As part of the solution , Akande recommended increased public education on proper waste disposal and the provision of adequate waste bins . <p> " The waste should be disposed of regularly . Sanitary inspectors and Environmental Health Officers should routinely inspect homes , workplaces , and public spaces to ensure proper waste collection and disposal . <p> " Relevant government ministries and agencies should be adequately funded to procure RORO bins , waste disposal vehicles , and other essential equipment . Homes , workplaces , and environments with poor waste disposal practices should be @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ on ResearchGate by Oludayo Abiodun and his team also highlighted the environmental and health dangers posed by poorly managed dumpsites . <p> The study noted that inadequate resources and technology often force rapidly growing cities such as Lagos to rely heavily on landfill sites for waste disposal . <p> However , it warned that poorly managed dumpsites could significantly harm both the environment and human health . <p> The researchers investigated the impact of the Olusosun dumpsite on groundwater quality by collecting and analysing 25 water samples from boreholes around the landfill . <p> Their findings revealed elevated levels of lead in all sampled locations , while more than half of the samples recorded zinc and copper concentrations above the standards set by the World Health Organisation and the Nigerian Standard for Drinking Water Quality . <p> Similarly , 52 per cent of the water samples had values outside acceptable limits . <p> The study stated , " Results revealed higher concentrations of physicochemical contaminants in locations closest to the landfill , with concentrations decreasing as the distance from the Olusosun landfill increased . " <p> The researchers recommended alternative @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ or proper treatment of groundwater before consumption . One of the deteriorating internal roads <p> LAWMA keeps mum <p> Efforts to obtain an official response from the Lagos State Waste Management Authority on conditions at the Olusosun dumpsite and the allegations raised by operators were unsuccessful . <p> When contacted on May 16 , 2026 , at about 11 a.m. , LAWMA 's Head of Public Affairs , Mr Mukaila Sanusi , asked our correspondent to send him a text message , which was done immediately . <p> However , after receiving no response , our correspondent made several follow-up calls . <p> Sanusi later answered one of the calls and said he would not comment on the matter that day but promised to do so the following day . <p> Follow-up calls to his phone on June 17 went unanswered , and he did not return any of them . <p> Further efforts to reach him on June 18 through text messages were also unsuccessful , as he neither replied to nor acknowledged the messages . <p> Our correspondent also contacted LAWMA 's former Public Relations Officer , Mrs @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the Lagos State Ministry of Information . <p> She subsequently provided the contact details of the agency 's new spokesperson and advised that further enquiries be directed to him . <p> The contact provided was still Sanusi 's . @@138317582 <h> How infrastructure-focused governance is robbing Nigeria of human capital development <p> Across Nigeria , signs of government activity are often measured in concrete and steel . Massive flyovers rise above busy intersections . New airports spring up in states with limited air traffic . Roads are commissioned amid fanfare , while government buildings undergo expensive renovations . For many politicians , these projects represent progress and evidence of performance . <p> This widening gap between physical infrastructure and human development is fueling a growing debate among economists , policy analysts and civil society advocates . While few dispute the importance of infrastructure , critics argue that governments at various levels are increasingly prioritising politically attractive projects while neglecting investments that directly improve the lives and productivity of citizens . <p> For many Nigerians , the contradiction is difficult to ignore . States battling salary arrears continue to announce multibillion-naira projects . Communities without functional primary healthcare centres watch flyovers being constructed nearby . Young graduates unable to secure employment see governments invest heavily in airports and road networks that offer little immediate impact on their economic prospects . <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ priorities have become skewed . <p> However , critics argue that many projects undertaken by state governments are driven less by development imperatives and more by political calculations . <p> " Physical infrastructure offers politicians a visible legacy . A governor can point to a bridge or airport as evidence of performance . Human capital investments , on the other hand , often take years to produce measurable outcomes and may not yield immediate political benefits , " a public affairs analyst said . <p> The result , analysts argue , is a governance culture that rewards visibility over impact . Roads and flyovers provide opportunities for commissioning ceremonies and public acclaim , while investments in education , healthcare and skills development often lack the same political appeal despite their long-term benefits . <p> The consequences are reflected in persistent unemployment , underemployment and multidimensional poverty . <p> Analysts argue that investments in education , healthcare , vocational training and entrepreneurship development generate some of the highest returns for sustainable economic growth . <p> " A healthy , educated and skilled population is more productive , innovative and capable of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , said . <p> Yet human capital development often remains secondary to infrastructure spending . <p> In many cases , state governments allocate billions of naira to roads and large-scale construction projects while dedicating comparatively little to teacher training , technical education , healthcare delivery and youth empowerment programmes . <p> The outcome is a development model that builds physical assets without adequately investing in the people expected to use them . <p> The numbers underscore the challenge . The World Bank 's Human Capital Index places Nigeria at 0.38 , meaning a child born in Nigeria today will be only 38 percent as productive as they could be with complete access to quality education and healthcare . <p> Data highlighted in the World Bank 's April 2026 Nigeria Development Update further emphasises that inclusive growth and sustainable improvements in living standards depend largely on how effectively the country invests in its people , beginning from early childhood development . <h> The cost of development without people <p> Across several states , efforts to boost internally generated revenue have translated into multiple taxes and levies on businesses and households already @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> Despite ongoing efforts by the federal government to reform the tax system , many states continue to rely on aggressive revenue collection strategies that place additional pressure on residents . <p> " If people can not afford food , healthcare or education , can we truly say development is taking place simply because a new flyover has been constructed ? " asks Alabi , earlier quoted . . <h> When roads are not enough <p> The debate is not about whether infrastructure should be built . Most experts agree that roads , bridges and transport networks remain critical for economic growth . <p> The concern is whether infrastructure has become an end in itself rather than a means to broader development . <p> Critics point to roads that deteriorate only a few years after completion despite enormous public expenditure . Weak oversight , poor maintenance culture and substandard execution often undermine the long-term value of such projects . <p> These realities raise important questions about sustainability , value for money and developmental impact . <p> Development experts argue that infrastructure delivers the greatest benefits when integrated into wider @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ creation and improved social services . <p> Without those connections , expensive projects risk becoming monuments to political ambition rather than catalysts for economic transformation . <h> Looking beyond concrete <p> Perhaps the strongest argument for rethinking development priorities can be found in Nigeria 's social indicators . <p> According to World Bank estimates , a child born in Cape Verde today can expect to live for about 76 years . Life expectancy stands at approximately 68 years in Namibia and Malawi , while Zambia has raised life expectancy to around 67 years despite once facing one of the world 's most severe HIV/AIDS crises . In Ghana and South Africa , the average life expectancy is about 66 years . <p> Nigeria ranks at the bottom among many of its democratic peers , with life expectancy estimated at only 55 years . <p> The story is similar in education . At the beginning of the Fourth Republic , Nigeria had an estimated seven million out-of-school children . Today , according to UNICEF , that figure has risen to approximately 18.3 million , representing one of the highest concentrations of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ statistics are millions of interrupted dreams . They are children displaced by insurgency in the North-East , students forced out of classrooms by insecurity in the North-West , and families unable to afford transportation , uniforms or learning materials . They are schools without sufficient teachers , classrooms or basic learning facilities . <p> These realities , analysts say , reveal a development crisis that can not be solved through infrastructure alone . <h> A call for grassroots-centred governance <p> Increasingly , experts are calling for a more people-centred approach to governance . They argue that development priorities should emerge from the actual needs of communities rather than the preferences of political leaders . <p> In many rural communities , residents are more concerned about access to clean water , quality healthcare , schools and agricultural support than the construction of grand projects . Urban residents often prioritise affordable housing , public transportation , security and employment opportunities . <p> Development , experts insist , should not be measured solely by the number of roads , bridges or airports commissioned . It should also be evaluated through improvements in literacy @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> The ultimate purpose of governance is not merely to erect structures but to improve human well-being . <h> Rethinking development priorities <p> As Nigeria confronts mounting economic and social challenges , the debate over infrastructure-focused governance is likely to become even more pronounced . <p> Physical infrastructure will continue to play a vital role in economic development . However , analysts argue that it must be matched by equally strong investments in people . <p> Roads and bridges may connect communities , but education , healthcare and skills development empower citizens to take advantage of those connections . <p> For a country with one of the world 's youngest populations , the most valuable asset is not concrete , asphalt or steel . It is its people . <p> Unless governments place human capital development at the centre of policy and spending decisions , Nigeria risks constructing impressive monuments while leaving behind the citizens those projects are meant to serve . <p> Taofeek Oyedokun is a correspondent at BusinessDay with years of experience reporting on political economy , public policy , migration , environment/climate change , and social @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of Lagos , he has also earned multiple professional certificates in journalism and media-related training . Known for his clear , data-driven reporting , Oyedokun covers a wide range of national and international socioeconomic issues , bringing depth , balance , and public-interest focus to his work . <p> BusinessDay , established in 2001 , is a media organisation headquartered in Lagos . @@138317599 <h> Abandoned in the Rubble : Oworonshoki 's unfinished story <p> The tiled floor where Joke Olowu once welcomed visitors into her home now serves as a bed for her and her five children . Her two houses are gone , her children have turned to scavenging for survival , while school has become a distant memory . Across Oworonshoki , hers is just one of hundreds of stories emerging from a demolition that residents say robbed them not only of their homes , but also of their dignity and future . TAOFEEK OYEDOKUN reports <p> As dusk settled over the waterfront community of Oworonshoki in Kosofe Local Government Area of Lagos State on a September night in 2025 , traders counted the proceeds from the day 's sales . Mothers prepared dinner over charcoal stoves . Children , exhausted from school and play , drifted into sleep beneath ceiling fans and mosquito nets . Men gathered outside their homes , exchanging stories about football , politics and the rising cost of living . <p> For decades , this had been the reality of life in one of Lagos ' @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of sacrifice ; tightly knit compounds where generations of families lived side by side ; neighbours who knew one another by name . <p> Then came the sound that would change everything . <p> At first , it was distant , the low rumble of heavy machinery breaking the silence of the night . Soon afterwards came the arrival of heavily armed security operatives . <p> Within hours , bulldozers were tearing through homes , shops and schools . Panic spread through the narrow streets as residents scrambled to rescue whatever pieces of their lives they could carry . <p> Some fled with mattresses balanced precariously on their heads . Others ran clutching babies in one arm and bundles of clothing in the other . Many escaped with nothing at all . <p> Months later , long after the machines departed , the destruction they left behind remains visible across Oworonshoki . <p> But while the broken walls and shattered concrete are impossible to ignore , another kind of devastation lies hidden beneath the rubble , the collapse of livelihoods , the interruption of childhoods , and the quiet despair @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ only home they had ever known . <h> The night Oworonshoki changed <p> " It started late last year , at Ojileri axis , towards the Third Mainland Bridge , at about 11:30 p.m. , 12:30 a.m. , they came in with their bulldozers , " recalled Daniel Ayomikun , a youth leader in the community . <p> " They started evicting people , gave them five minutes to pack out their belongings . " <p> The calls for help came quickly . Residents contacted youth leaders in panic as word spread that homes were being brought down . <p> " We started putting calls across at midnight and we confirmed it was true , " Ayomikun said . " Then we , the youth , moved down there and we saw with our eyes that they were evicting people and demolishing houses around 12:30 a.m . " <p> According to him , the operation did not end that night . " They started that pattern for like a week . They would work only at night . " <p> He alleged that security personnel were accompanied by thugs who @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ task force also , they are like hired thugs , " he said . " They came with thugs and hooligans . They would start selling people 's valuables , home appliances and the likes . It was really annoying . " <p> As bulldozers advanced through the community , panic gave way to disbelief . Residents watched helplessly as structures built over decades disappeared within minutes . <p> Families were separated in the confusion . Children cried . The elderly struggled to move . Entire lives were reduced to debris before sunrise . <h> Living among ruins <p> Demolitions in parts of Oworonshoki have been gradually ongoing since 2023 , displacing hundreds of households . <p> Earlier exercises in Araba and Koka were resisted by residents . Families complained about inadequate compensation , destruction of property and poor coordination by authorities . But the large-scale demolition of 2025 altered the community irreversibly . <p> A recent visit to Oworonshoki by BusinessDay revealed scars too deep to ignore . Entire stretches of land that once accommodated vibrant residential compounds now resemble abandoned construction sites . <p> Tarpaulins have been tied @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ into makeshift roofs . Wooden planks and recovered blocks serve as sleeping platforms . <p> These fragile structures have become home . When rain pours through the night , residents endure . When the afternoon heat becomes unbearable beneath improvised shelters , they endure that too . Many simply have no alternative . <p> Lagos is in the grip of one of its worst housing affordability crises in recent history . For displaced families who have lost both homes and livelihoods , renting elsewhere is impossible . <p> Those who remain in Oworonshoki have effectively become refugees in their own community . <p> " We sleep where our house used to be , I now sleep outside with my children . Even when it rains , we have nowhere to go , " Omowunmi , a mother of two , lamented . <p> Her children no longer attend school . The petty business that sustained her family collapsed with the demolition . <p> " The government should provide accommodation or return our lands to us , " she pleaded . Rotimi Sunday , a former landlord who now lives on @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ <p> Nearby , Rotimi Sunday sat surrounded by fragments of a life he thought was secure . <p> " My father lived here . I was born here . My children were raised here . Now we have nowhere else to go , " he told BusinessDay . Around him , children played barefoot among broken blocks and debris . <p> Sunday inherited the family property from his father . He accused the Oworonshoki monarch , Oba Saliu Babatunde , of playing a major role in the events that displaced them . <p> " When Oba Saliu was crowned , we were all happy and felicitated with him not knowing that he had a plan to make us homeless , " he said . <p> " They said by pasting quit notices on our houses , asking us to come to the government secretariat . From the secretariat , we were directed to the monarch . " <p> He recalled the meeting that followed . " When we finally met him , he asked us to renovate our houses , saying that he wanted the community to fit into the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and renovated our buildings , but they still demolished them . " <p> Today , he sleeps outside . His business has suffered . His children have been scattered across Lagos . Their education has been disrupted . He wants compensation or restoration . Anything that resembles justice . <p> The scene feels less like a residential neighbourhood and more like the aftermath of a natural disaster . Except this disaster was man-made . <h> A community erased <p> Residents say the demolition unfolded in phases . The operation began around areas close to the Third Mainland Bridge , where hundreds of structures were flattened during overnight exercises involving heavily armed security personnel . <p> Many residents were caught completely unprepared . Some awoke to discover demolition teams already outside their homes . Others say they were given little opportunity to remove belongings before bulldozers moved in . <p> By the end of 2025 , hundreds of houses had been reduced to rubble despite protests by residents , interventions by activists and ongoing legal disputes . <p> The demolitions triggered outrage across Lagos . <p> Femi Falana , human rights @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ exercise despite an existing restraining order issued by a Lagos State High Court . <p> Justice Adegboyega Balogun had granted an interim order restraining further demolitions within parts of the affected community while disputes concerning compensation and ownership were being addressed . <p> Residents believed the court order would protect them . Instead , many say the demolitions continued . <p> For affected families , it reinforced a painful conclusion : the institutions meant to safeguard their rights had failed them . <p> Ayomikun , earlier quoted , said the consequences have been severe . <p> " This thing has really affected a lot of families . Many have died , " he said . " There were about 26 people sent to prison for about a month for a peaceful protest . " <p> He also alleged that an infant died during one of the operations . " While the task force was working in December , they tear-gassed an eight-month-old baby and she was choked . She died in the process . " <h> Childhood interrupted <p> Among the most devastating consequences of the demolition is the impact @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ education and uncertain futures . <p> Parents who lost homes often lost their sources of income as well . School fees became impossible to pay and food itself became uncertain . <p> Many children who once attended nearby schools now spend their days wandering through demolished sites . <p> Teenagers have turned to scavenging . Armed with sacks and makeshift hooks , they search refuse dumps and construction waste for plastic bottles that can be sold to recycling agents . The earnings are meagre , but every naira matters . He also pointed to cholera outbreaks in parts of Eastern and Southern Africa , alongside worsening air @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , climate change is already reshaping disease patterns across Africa , with a growing share of public health emergencies now directly linked to environmental disruption . <p> He stressed that planetary health is not merely an academic idea but a core survival framework for the continent . <p> He outlined five pillars of sovereignty : data ownership , African-led research priorities , local analytical capacity , control of AI infrastructure , and policy independence in the application of scientific evidence . <p> Without these , he warned , Africa risks becoming " a consumer of insights it does not control " . <p> While acknowledging the promise of artificial intelligence in outbreak prediction , genomic surveillance , and climate-health modelling , Ilesanmi cautioned against uncritical adoption . <p> " We are applying foreign intelligence to local realities , " he said , noting that many AI systems used in Africa are trained on external datasets and assumptions that may not reflect local conditions . <p> He argued that this creates a structural imbalance in which Africa relies on tools it does not meaningfully shape . <p> The public health physicist @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ systems , including chronic underinvestment in research and development , fragmented health data systems , weak computing infrastructure , and the continued migration of skilled data and AI professionals . <p> He added that heavy reliance on external funding and digital platforms further deepens dependency . <p> " The future of planetary health is not only technological ; it is moral and political , " he said . " Africa must decide : remain a data consumer or become a data sovereign . Africa 's next public health transformation will depend not only on the availability of data but also on who owns and controls it . " 
@@138344609 <p> Even before President Bola Ahmed Tinubu assented to the Electricity Act on 6 June 2023 , the plot to strangle this cherished baby had begun . Barely two years after the Act gave statutory recognition to the most important restructuring of Nigeria 's electricity sector since privatisation , the Senate 's Electricity Act ( Amendment ) Bill , 2025 , was finally unveiled in late 2025 . It is presented in reassuring language : coordination , clarification , emerging issues , sectoral financing , consumer protection and transitional certainty . But in electricity , as in politics , the title of a thing is not always the truth of it . <p> The truth is simple . This bill seeks to claw back , by mere legislative drafting , what the Constitution deliberately gave to the states by constitutional amendment . It seeks to restore , in new vocabulary , the unitary electricity governance architecture that failed Nigeria for decades . It seeks to make the Nigerian Electricity Regulatory Commission , NERC , the master and commander of state electricity regulators , not the counterpart , collaborator and mentor @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ federal agencies in spaces where the Constitution no longer gives the National Assembly power to legislate . It seeks to make state electricity markets appear autonomous while ensuring that the key regulatory levers remain in Abuja . <p> This bill is therefore a bad idea . It is an assault on the logic of the 5th Alteration to the 1999 Constitution , 2023 , and the Electricity Act , 2023 ; on the fragile investor confidence now gradually forming around state electricity markets ; and on the only reform pathway that offers Nigeria a realistic chance of escaping the repeated failure of its electricity sector , especially the distribution side . <h> A little history <p> The Electricity Act , 2023 , is not an accident . It is the statutory expression of a deliberate constitutional shift . In 2019 , a group of governors questioned , at the National Economic Council under then Vice President Yemi Osinbajo , the abject performance of electricity Discos after the 2013 privatisation . A committee of six governors constituted to examine the matter concluded that unless states took responsibility for their energy security @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ letters . The first step was a constitutional amendment to remove the constraints on the powers of states to make laws for electricity within their territories . <p> This led to the move to amend Paragraph 14 of the Concurrent Legislative List , Part II , Second Schedule to the 1999 Constitution , ratified by the States and assented to by President Buhari as part of the 5th Alteration to the 1999 Constitution in March 2023 . It removed the old limitation that confined state legislative competence to electricity areas " not covered by a national grid system " . That phrase mattered . It was added in the 1999 Constitution to a similar Paragraph 14 inherited from the 1979 Constitution . This vague phrase was then used to justify the fiction that once electricity touched the national grid , its regulation belonged to NERC alone . The result was a centralised electricity sector in a federal country that reduced states to spectators in a matter vital to their individual economic wellbeing . <p> The 5th Alteration ended that constitutional aberration . The Electricity Act , the first legislation assented @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . It is important to recall that the Act was not originally conceived to be reformist . It was intended to consolidate existing electricity-sector statutes and federal agencies and reinforce the powers of the minister and the various federal regulators ; it aimed to re-establish , much like the intent of the Petroleum Industry Act . <p> Its reform essence -- Sections 2(2) and 230(2) -- ( 9 ) -- was inserted through the efforts of the Nigerian Governors ' Forum , led then by Governor Kayode Fayemi , working with governors from across the six geopolitical zones . The House of Representatives , under Speaker Femi Gbajabiamila , ensured these reform provisions went into the Senate version . The harmonised bill became the Electricity Act , 2023 . This history matters because state electricity markets are not an afterthought , a personal agenda , or an accidental drafting outcome . They were the deliberate product of constitutional amendment , states ' ratification , legislative negotiation and presidential assent -- precisely how laws are made . The purveyors of this amendment bill appear to have forgotten , or chosen to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is clear . States can now legislate exclusively and fully for electricity within their territories . They can establish electricity markets and state regulators . They can design policy and legal frameworks for whatever electricity activity is carried on within their boundaries . <p> This bill is a constitutional law non-starter . <p> State markets do not mean Nigeria no longer has a national grid , a national system operator , interstate trade , cross-border transactions or federal responsibilities . It means only that Nigeria 's electricity reform can no longer be delivered from a single regulatory command centre . It also means , and we must be clear-eyed about this , that the Amendment Bill has a serious constitutional law problem from the onset . Paragraph 13 of Part II of the Second Schedule gives the National Assembly power over generation and transmission in relation to the national grid and over interstate and international electricity matters . It is careful about what it says . It does not refer to " electricity distribution " or confer on the National Assembly even a vague or general power over electricity distribution @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ This has consequences . Paragraph 14 , on the other hand , in the clearest terms confers on states the power to make laws for electricity within their territories . As simple as that , the boundaries are crystal clear . <p> It is elementary that the National Assembly can not , by ordinary legislation , reinsert limitations that the Constitution has removed . It can not , by calling a thing coordination , recover powers it no longer has . It can not , by expanding NERC or NEMSA 's role , amend the Constitution through the back door . The National Assembly does not have the constitutional power to enact this Electricity Act ( Amendment ) Bill to the extent that it touches on electricity distribution or anything connected therewith and the conduct of generation , transmission and ancillary business solely within the boundaries of a state . Yet that is what the Amendment Bill attempts . <p> So , put simply , most , if not all , of this amendment bill is liable to be declared unconstitutional by a court of law . This is where @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , we should also discuss the substance of the bill 's proposed amendments . <h> Seven hills to climb <p> First , the proposed amendment to Section 2 . On its face , it appears to restate the constitutional powers conferred on states . It says State Houses of Assembly may make laws on generation , transmission , system operation , distribution , supply and retail electricity within state boundaries . So far , so good . But then comes the proviso . State laws must not conflict with federal provisions relating to the national grid , the National Wholesale Electricity Market , technical standards , operational codes , consumer protection , antitrust , and climate change mitigation and adaptation . That provision negates the express provisions of Paragraphs 13 and 14 of the Concurrent Legislative List . By what power can the Senate insert it without another constitutional amendment ? None . <p> National standards are important . No serious state electricity market should operate unsafe networks , undermine grid discipline or create regulatory chaos . The problem is that this proviso is not drafted as cooperation . It @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ that may arise wholly within a state market -- consumer protection , technical standards , operational codes and competition rules within state markets -- and places them under a federal supremacy formula . That is not federalism . It is unsubstantiated arrogance and a superiority complex disguised as conditional delegation . The States did not receive their electricity lawmaking powers from NERC , NEMSA or the minister of power . They received them from the Constitution . Federal legislation can not downgrade unconditional constitutional authority into delegated statutory authority . <p> Second , regulatory confusion . Section 63(7) of the Electricity Act currently preserves the integrity of state licensing and market operations in respect of mini-grids . The bill proposes to delete it . It looks like clearing up ambiguity but is actually the direct opposite . In a sector where investors need to know who licenses , who regulates , who approves tariffs , who enforces performance standards , who resolves disputes and who bears political responsibility , ambiguity is poisonous . State markets are already difficult enough to build . Apart from law , institutions , people , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ To delete a provision that affirms state licensing authority is to invite uncertainty precisely when certainty is most needed . <p> Third , the proposed transition regime . This is the heart of the Bill . Proposed Sections 230A to 230C introduce a 12-month period within which States must meet transition conditions , with a possible six-month extension at NERC 's discretion . If a state fails to satisfy NERC , NERC may determine interim arrangements . NERC is also positioned to review state progress , adjust implementation frameworks and determine regulatory boundaries . <p> This is not a transition . It is an imposition . What makes these provisions even more curious is that what is to be transitioned to state regulators by NERC is essentially intellectual property , all of which is in NERC 's absolute control . Obviously , it is NERC that has the obligation to respond to a state seeking transfer of authority with a programme for the transfer of that IP . Instead , the amendment makes an unclear reference to " conditions precedent stipulated under the Principal Act " and imposes a compulsory @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ these " conditions precedent " . <p> The Electricity Act already created a transition process , which the new sections do not delete or replace . That means the Act would , when amended , provide for two diametrically opposed transition processes . Under Section 230(2) -- ( 4 ) , a state that has enacted its electricity law and established its regulator with a governing body and staff may engage NERC and the NCP and request the transfer of regulatory oversight and the establishment of an electricity distribution subsidiary in the state . Following this engagement , NERC issues a transfer order any time within six months following notification by the state . This is sensible because states are not all at the same point . Some have moved quickly . Some will move slowly . Some have policy capacity but no regulator . Some have laws but no staff . Some have strong political will but weak technical support . Some will initially rely on embedded expertise from NERC , licensees , consultants or development partners . This is how institutional reform works . <p> The proposed 12-month-plus-six-month @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ another that no state needs . The real challenge is not that states are refusing to transition . It is that they must build capacity responsibly . They need market rules , licensing frameworks , tariff methodologies , inspection arrangements , consumer redress processes and grid-interface rules . They must decide how to deal with legacy DisCos , embedded generation , industrial clusters , rural electrification , franchising , mini-grids , state assets , federal assets and commercial off-take . Health authorities also detected multiple outbreaks among non-human primates , an important indicator of ongoing viral circulation . <p> The mosquito-borne disease remains endemic in tropical regions of Africa and South America . Although many infections are mild , severe cases can cause jaundice , internal bleeding , and organ failure , with fatality rates reaching up to 50% . <p> The WHO warned that unvaccinated populations remain particularly vulnerable . In Africa @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ estimated at just 65% in 2024 , below the 80% threshold considered necessary for strong population protection . <p> The Americas continue to experience the effects of a major resurgence that began in late 2024 . The region recorded 241 cases and 100 deaths between late 2024 and early 2025 , marking an eightfold increase compared with the previous year . Colombia has been among the countries most affected during the current wave . <p> Health experts say a combination of environmental and social factors is driving transmission , including favourable mosquito breeding conditions , population movement , and urban expansion into forested areas where the virus naturally circulates . <p> While no imported cases have been reported outside Africa and the Americas , the WHO warned that increasing international travel and the spread of mosquito vectors could create opportunities for future outbreaks elsewhere . <p> The agency is urging countries to strengthen disease surveillance , laboratory testing , and vaccination programmes . @@138461728 <p> The Lagos State Government has reaffirmed its commitment to building a climate-resilient future through deliberate policies , strategic partnerships , and sustainable urban planning that protects the environment while accommodating the city 's rapid growth . <p> The State Commissioner for the Environment and Water Resources , Mr. Tokunbo Wahab , gave the reassurance at the Lagos Policy Dialogue on Climate Adaptation in the Gulf of Guinea : Building Preferred Futures . <p> According to the commissioner , Lagos has moved beyond discussions and is actively implementing concrete climate adaptation measures . <p> He said the State unveiled its Lagos Climate Adaptation and Resilience Plan during the 28th United Nations Climate Change Conference ( COP28 ) in Dubai , and has since been aligning its environmental and developmental programmes with the objectives of the plan . <p> " As a State , we have gone beyond talks ; Everything we do is now centred around protecting our ecosystem , strengthening climate resilience and driving adaptation initiatives that will safeguard the future of Lagos , " Wahab stated . <p> The Commissioner noted that Lagos ' recognition as a pilot @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ role in climate governance and its commitment to developing practical solutions to environmental challenges . <p> " The future of Lagos and the Gulf of Guinea depends on the choices we make today ; Climate adaptation is no longer a future aspiration ; it is an urgent necessity that requires bold action , collaboration and a shared commitment to protecting our environment for generations to come , " he said . <p> He observed that as one of Africa 's fastest-growing megacities , Lagos faces enormous pressure from rapid urbanisation and population growth , making it imperative to pursue development without undermining environmental sustainability . <p> " We must ensure that we do not sacrifice climate resilience and the environment on the altar of urbanisation ; @@138470515 <h> Explainer : What Nigerians need to know about the State Police Bill <p> Nigeria has moved a step closer to ending nearly six decades of a centrally controlled policing system following the passage of the State Police constitutional amendment bill by the National Assembly . <p> The Senate approved the bill on Wednesday , following its earlier passage by the House of Representatives , marking what many have described as one of the country 's most significant security reforms since independence . While the legislation is yet to become law , its passage opens the constitutional pathway for the establishment of state police across the federation . <p> The move comes amid growing concerns over rising insecurity , including banditry , kidnapping , communal violence and other crimes that many stakeholders argue have exposed the limitations of a single federally controlled police force . <p> Why the bill matters <p> The existing policing structure is rooted in Section 214 of the 1999 Constitution , which establishes a single Nigeria Police Force under the command of an Inspector-General of Police appointed by the President . <p> Although governors are constitutionally @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ they do not exercise operational control over police commissioners deployed to their states , creating what critics describe as a disconnect between those responsible for security and those with the authority to direct policing operations . <p> Supporters of the State Police Bill argue that the centralised model has struggled to respond effectively to increasingly localised security threats because officers are often posted to communities where they have limited knowledge of the terrain , language or local dynamics . <p> The bill seeks to decentralise policing while maintaining national standards and federal oversight . <p> Under the proposed arrangement , the federal police will continue to handle national security responsibilities such as terrorism , cybercrime , organised crime , protection of federal institutions and policing of the Federal Capital Territory . <p> State police services , on the other hand , will focus on maintaining law and order within their respective states . <p> One of the most significant changes proposed by the bill is the strengthening of the National Police Council . <p> Beyond government officials , the council will include representatives of civil society organisations , the Nigerian @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ National Human Rights Commission and traditional rulers . <p> The council will set national policing standards , supervise both federal and state police services , certify states before they establish police services and resolve disputes arising from policing operations . <p> How commissioners will be appointed <p> The bill provides that state police commissioners will be appointed by governors but must first receive confirmation from their respective state Houses of Assembly . <p> More importantly , commissioners are empowered to challenge directives they believe are unlawful by referring such orders directly to the National Police Council , whose decision will be binding . This provision is intended to provide an institutional check against political interference in policing . <p> Perhaps the biggest concern surrounding state police has been the possibility that governors could use the force to intimidate political opponents or suppress dissent . The bill attempts to address these fears through several safeguards . <p> It expressly prohibits the arrest or detention of individuals solely for criticising the government or holding opposing political views . <p> It also establishes an independent mechanism allowing commissioners to reject unlawful directives from @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ <p> To reduce political manipulation , the legislation protects the tenure of both the Inspector-General of Police and state police commissioners by requiring legislative supermajorities before they can be removed from office . <p> The proposal also creates 37 Police Service Commissions -- one federal and one in each state -- to oversee recruitment , appointments and disciplinary matters . <p> Another safeguard requires states to meet nationally prescribed standards before establishing police services , while the use of weapons will remain subject to federal regulation , with states restricted largely to light arms for routine policing . <p> When can the federal government intervene ? Despite devolving policing powers to the states , the bill preserves federal authority under clearly defined circumstances . <p> Federal police may intervene if a state 's security situation completely collapses , if a governor formally requests assistance , or if a state 's police service becomes inoperable with the approval of the National Police Council . <p> The intention is to decentralise policing without weakening national security coordination . <p> The bill is not yet law <p> Although the National Assembly has passed @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ police become operational . <p> The amendment must first be approved by at least 24 of the 36 state Houses of Assembly , as required for constitutional alterations . It must then receive presidential assent . <p> Following that , the National Assembly will have to enact a comprehensive State Police Act covering funding arrangements , training standards and operational coordination between federal and state police services . <p> Individual states will also be required to pass their own laws establishing state police institutions and service commissions before recruitment can begin . <p> Finally , the National Police Council must certify that each participating state has met the required standards before officers can be deployed . <p> The road ahead <p> The passage of the State Police Bill represents one of Nigeria 's most ambitious attempts to reform its security architecture since the return to democratic rule . Supporters believe decentralising policing will improve intelligence gathering , strengthen community policing and enable faster responses to local security challenges . <p> However , analysts caution that the success of the reform will ultimately depend on adequate funding , professional recruitment , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ prevent political abuse . <p> For now , the constitutional door has been opened . Whether state police become a transformative security solution or another institutional experiment will depend on how faithfully the remaining legal , political and operational processes are implemented . <p> Taofeek Oyedokun is a correspondent at BusinessDay with years of experience reporting on political economy , public policy , migration , environment/climate change , and social justice . A graduate of Political Science from the University of Lagos , he has also earned multiple professional certificates in journalism and media-related training . Known for his clear , data-driven reporting , Oyedokun covers a wide range of national and international socioeconomic issues , bringing depth , balance , and public-interest focus to his work . <p> BusinessDay , established in 2001 , is a media organisation headquartered in Lagos . After six years of service , she returned to the headquarters , where she handled key bilateral and multilateral assignments . <p> She later left public service in 2002 to work in the private sector as a management consultant and author . However , her return to diplomacy came in 2016 when she was nominated into Nigeria 's non-career ambassadorial cadre . <p> On August 30 , 2017 , she was appointed Nigeria 's Ambassador to the Republic of Ireland , with concurrent accreditation to Iceland . Her tenure in Dublin marked a defining phase of her diplomatic career , as she earned a reputation for innovation in consular services , investment promotion , and citizen engagement . Her performance was widely regarded as one of the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ paved the way for a historic appointment in January 2021 as Nigeria 's first female Ambassador to the United States of America . @@138525578 <h> Tomato Prices To Crash From August -- Traders <p> Tomato prices currently weighing on Nigerian households may begin to ease from August as the wet-season harvest brings fresh supplies to market , the Tomatoes Growers and Processors Association of Nigeria ( TOPAN ) has said . <p> The Kaduna State Chairman of TOPAN , Rabiu Zuntu , gave the assurance on Sunday , blaming the prevailing spike on the seasonal gap between the close of dry-season production and the commencement of rainy-season cultivation . He said fresh tomatoes from farms are expected to enter markets from late July , triggering a significant price decline from August . <p> " We projected that the increase in tomato prices would last until the end of July when wet-season harvesting begins . From August , prices will begin to decline considerably because fresh supplies will enter the market , " Zuntu said . <p> The price surge , which began in early May , has seen a 50kg basket rise from between N18,000 and N20,000 in January to between N100,000 and N130,000 , depending on variety . A 25kg crate now sells @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> The development comes against the backdrop of a deepening structural crisis in Nigeria 's tomato sector . THE WHISTLER recently reported that Kano State -- one of the country 's foremost tomato-producing zones -- has been unable to translate its production advantage into processing or export revenue , spending over $400m annually importing tomato paste that its own farms could supply . A 2025 outbreak of Tuta absoluta , a leaf-mining moth , put 4,621 hectares of Kano tomato farms at risk , compounding losses already running into billions of naira . <p> Zuntu said the adoption of flood-resistant tomato varieties had enabled many farmers to sustain cultivation into the rainy season , bringing expected harvest dates forward . Without the improved varieties , he said , consumers might have waited until October or November for prices to ease . <p> He attributed the recurring May-to-June shortage to climate change , citing unpredictable rainfall and extreme heat that had disrupted production in major tomato-growing states including Kaduna and Kano . <p> Calling for wider access to heat-tolerant seeds , Zuntu urged the government and private investors to establish @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ structural gap <p> THE WHISTLER 's investigation identified as central to the sector 's instability . He said improved storage would allow farmers to preserve surplus produce for several weeks , bridging supply gaps between seasons and dampening the sharp price swings that have made tomatoes a recurring flashpoint of food inflation . <p> He appealed for sustained government support through the provision of seeds , fertilisers and agrochemicals , warning that without long-term investment in the tomato value chain , farmers would continue to oscillate between bumper-season losses and lean-season scarcity , while consumers bore the consequences at the market . <p> Advertisement <p> Zuntu acknowledged that current high prices had helped some farmers recover losses from previous seasons but said the gains were unsustainable without deliberate policy action .

This perspective is rooted in centuries of knowledge , craftsmanship , circularity , and community-led innovation . It demonstrates how @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ resilient fashion future . <h> Reclaiming value across Africa 's fashion industry <p> At the heart of the Manifesto is a call for ownership and value . For generations , Africa has supplied the materials , craftsmanship and creativity that shape global fashion while capturing little of the value it helps create . Today , Africa exports approximately$15 billion of raw textiles each year , yet imports more than $23 billion of finished clothing and footwear . The Manifesto argues that this is not a talent gap but an ownership gap , which needs to be filled . <p> The Manifesto was developed following a series of convenings , workshops , consultations , and stakeholder engagements led by Lagos Fashion Week and The Earthshot Prize . It reflects the collective thinking of the African Fashion Coalition , a community of African fashion leaders , sustainability advocates , researchers , educators , artisans , and entrepreneurs . Building on the Manifesto Lab convened in April 2026 , the document was further refined through ongoing consultation and collaboration across the ecosystem , resulting in a shared framework for a sustainable fashion future @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Manifesto recognises that many of the principles now being championed as solutions for a regenerative fashion future have long existed across African fashion systems and cultural practices . These include repair , reuse , stewardship , local production , resource efficiency , and intergenerational knowledge transfer . <p> It is structured around ten interconnected pillars spanning cultural heritage , circularity , inclusive prosperity , intellectual property protection , waste justice , local manufacturing , regenerative innovation , market access , infrastructure development and conscious consumption . These pillars provide a framework for strengthening the systems , partnerships , and investments needed to support a more regenerative , inclusive , and resilient fashion future across the continent . <p> " The Blueprint for a Regenerative Fashion Future reflects a shared vision that is embedded in African realities . For generations , Africa has supplied the materials , craftsmanship , and creativity that shape global fashion while capturing little of the value it helps create . Individuals and organisations across the fashion ecosystem are invited to read and sign the manifesto here and to join the coalition in carrying it from intention to adoption .
